# Native game gap closure delivery plan

## Outcome and integration base

Close the source-backed S1, S2 and S3K audit gaps through independently owned
implementation slices, then qualify and integrate their composed behavior.
The pre-task destination is `develop` at
`94c40570a15a11b70caf7456e9b0d0a8eec8a9b4`. The lead's aggregate branch is
`feature/ai-native-gap-swarm-20261022`; the main workspace remains on `develop`.
Worker branches stay local. No public release or release-only full suite is
part of this task.

The original audit used executable wiring, actual assertions, disassembly and
verified ROM bytes. Registration inventories establish dispatch breadth, not
complete subtype, route, rendering or timing fidelity. Newly discovered causal
defects belong to the owning slice; unknown behavior stays explicitly unknown.

The peer's draft PR 223 is a separate integration dependency. Its feature-branch
commits are not main-workspace integration. Both leads exchange exact source,
validation and integration windows before mutating the shared destination.

## Shared surfaces and composition

- S2 presentation owns only `GameLoop.startEndingFade` and `doEnterEnding`,
  using an internal ending-entry capability; the public ending API stays intact.
- S2 chain owns the measured special-stage return lifecycle question and
  post-movement/pre-animation Super phase. Native modes owns title dispatch,
  participant input and world/view selection. Their `SpriteManager` changes
  must compose by method and phase, not by choosing a file wholesale.
- S1 lifecycle owns private checkpoint background transport, its internal
  accessor/provider bridge and registered S1 adapter. Preserve the actual
  parallax provider retained by the runtime; a newly returned provider is not
  its live state owner.
- S1 chain owns the narrow borrowed-execution retirement parent-slot correction
  in `ObjectManager`. Capsule allocation uses existing APIs and introduces no
  competing manager change.
- Sprite limits owns gameplay SAT/HUD composition and native footprint loaders.
  The S2 stage owner consumes its H32 API while preserving the stage's native
  slot creation/order. Native modes consumes the same presentation contracts.
- Narrow S3K owns internal event access to the shared Nemesis FIFO and module
  wiring. Preserve fresh-title six-pattern service, ordinary three-pattern
  service, prior queued work, one service per opportunity and one rewind owner.
- Audio evidence owns only the configured-headless launch backend retention
  seam in `Engine`. The options consumer owns the native-request music remap
  in `AudioManager`; ordinary engine playlist IDs retain their meaning.
- The peer reserves glide/Air movement semantics and terrain helpers. Native
  modes' new public mode exports and the peer's Air-rule exports require
  composed descriptor/version/signature-pin validation, not a textual pin win.
- S1 chain owns the separate `CameraRules` grounded-roll recenter gate and
  minimal `doRollSpeed` consumer. Native modes composes that signature with
  its mode exports and the peer's Air-rule field. No glide region is shared.
- S2 chain owns the corrected control/global Super phase split, including
  package-private base operations and the world runtime's post-object palette
  dispatch. Global palette work must run during hurt/death and release control
  after animation; views and derived rewind rendering must not advance it.
- Assertion repair's follow-up owns an internal native display-submission
  queue, late SST resolution and adjacent rewind registrations. S1 chain owns
  Bomb calls; sprite limits owns SAT replay and reserved-ring emission. Preserve
  duplicates and admitted deleted pointers without capturing callback identities.
- Sprite limits owns stencil coverage for native sprite winners, including
  terrain-hidden winning pixels, and native ring draw ordering. Native modes'
  collection/streaming changes stay separate. Shadow/highlight operators need
  their actual ROM art and operator semantics within the admitted SAT.
- Audio evidence owns scoped recorded-input ownership and the logical Start
  host-pause and native-pause read gates. Physical keyboard pause remains
  independent. Its production observations disproved the earlier audio rejection
  hypothesis; no shared producer/chip mutation follows from that hypothesis.

Temporary task IDs, exact workspaces, expiry, resumption IDs and live checkpoint
handles are recorded in the lead-owned temporary-agent registry outside Git.
Workers update only their own checkpoints. Capacity failures resume the same
owned session/worktree; preserve uncommitted work and foreign submodules.

## Verification and delivery

1. Each worker reproduces its actual failure, implements the native behavior,
   runs focused meaningful regressions and applicable guards, then provides a
   local commit, exact command, source state, failures/skips and remaining limits.
   Retain queued jobs; do not launch duplicate unchanged selections.
2. The lead reviews and composes committed milestones in this isolated tree.
   Later edits require affected checks. Source-correct isolated changes that
   expose compensation remain explicit until the combined path is repaired.
3. Inspect `run_categories.py --base 94c40570a15a11b70caf7456e9b0d0a8eec8a9b4`
   on the final composition. State candidate count, expected cost and stopping
   rule; run actual Java 25/Lua 5.4/PowerShell preflight, then one combined
   change-based smoke selection and affected native/domain checks. Shared
   changes use the complete selected smoke categories; no manual narrowing.
4. Attribute disputed failures with bounded matched tests on base and current
   state. The peer's historical 14 ordinary and four guard failures are neither
   a current baseline nor waived regressions. New or worsened failures block
   integration; unchanged attributed baseline failures do not.
5. Rendering uses surfaceless EGL with display variables unset and known
   no-device audio. Native trace/audio evidence requires exact ROM, movie,
   producer, service boundary and completed coverage. Comparison rows never
   supply gameplay state. The dedicated timing contract remains the only
   restricted readiness/input-admission exception.
6. Coordinate the destination window with the peer, fetch/fast-forward its
   current branch without switching it, reconcile upstream intent, complete
   required affected post-integration checks, and push only that destination.
   Keep incomplete scopes open; do not describe focused or partial evidence as
   a full-suite, complete-game or all-route pass.
7. Inspect and acknowledge category diagnostics after reading their summaries.
   Preserve compact decisions and rejected-approach evidence in existing task
   artifacts; remove consumed raw diagnostics. After successful verification
   and push, account for every owned change before removing fully merged
   worktrees/branches. Preserve unknown and unmerged work.

Graphics/background owned four paths; object/world/rewind/ring ownership five;
S1 scroll/FZ eight; S3K CNZ/SSZ/HCZ four; terrain one; parent prose, hook mirrors
and guidance eight. Dependency hunks followed their actual receiving owners:

- Native sprite calls retain the authenticated slot/bucket ledger, capacity 63,
  duplicates, admission ordinals, raw native-pass presence, current physical owner
  resolution and ID-zero refusal. Active-object iteration, sorting or deduplication
  cannot reconstruct accepted native calls. Directed replacement runs once at the
  qualified native-entry draw point, including directed children. Explicit held
  Puppet/Pose pictures use a separate non-native suffix, still charged to actual
  SAT piece/row/pixel budgets. Raw accepted addresses suppress picture and legacy
  external-anchor fallback even for ID-zero/orphan entries; this extra external
  filter is an intentional receiving change, not byte-unchanged transport. Typed
  external qualification, current-owner precedence, accepted repeats and the
  original unsubmitted legacy lane remain. No persistent admission bank was added.
- Masks are immutable call-time metadata. Default low/high masks remain 15/0;
  ordinary explicit 7 stays 7 independently of directing leases. Incoming tests
  that selected ordinary 7 but expected 15 instead establish the default through
  the priority setter and retain a separate explicit-mask freeze discriminator.
  Last-visual attachment/clipping replaces the leaf in the active fragment or
  ordinary list, preserving operation, ghost, subject and fragment topology.
  Empty active fragments and unsupported completed groups fail closed; no mutable
  identity-map mask owner or silent group-only attachment was retained.
- Cinematic backgrounds capture bounded full 16-bit retained images, independent
  foreground/background activation, explicit inactive authority, revision plus
  image-generation identity, private residency, frozen arena bounds and vertical
  wrap policy. Copies contain no live plane/provider authority and do not inherit
  native residency keys. View, camera and tile/slice paths restore private lookup
  textures, pending native residency and owned state on success or failure. The
  ordinary arena-mask method is byte-identical. Native-publication frozen build
  data is distinct from the existing one-time entry mode resolution under the
  unchanged native camera/frame; arbitrary contributors are not claimed pure.
  Replacing that entry operation with published sprite-scroll state was rejected.
- Removal captures an ephemeral exact-receiver/registered-owner token before the
  first unregister, then invokes only that receiver's removal notification at its
  original later point. Moving unload/notification, restoring registration, an
  ownerless router call after unregister, class/slot matching or a new retained
  ownership bank were rejected. Only removal may elide an already established
  same-router/same-owner boundary. Normal null-owner/absent-boundary direct calls,
  ambient owner restoration, A-to-B reentry, graph order and the native full-delete
  single outer callback boundary remain; its removal fault skips later unload and
  is attributed once. Materialization wraps the existing post-construction writes
  without changing failed-construction or slot-release timing. Word2C still
  distinguishes unknown cells from known residue and rejects unprojected owners.
- S1 projections coexist with checkpoint word transport, complete fractional/cloud
  rewind state and SBZ3's independent Labyrinth handler. Sampling does not advance
  native clocks. FZ visual release uses only acquired identities and the existing
  forced-mask setter to zero; clearing forced input through the broader latch-reset
  helper or rewriting native UP/zero/logical history was rejected. CNZ retains its
  native hidden/Y-word phase and separate actual control acquisitions; no removed
  `playerCaptured` field was restored. HCZ ending release belongs to the retained
  root's actual control/CPU owners, not a resurrected capsule geyser handoff. The
  central acquired-player identity codec covers the resulting owner sets. SSZ
  keeps positive-control solid permission and signed-negative refusal. Terrain
  probes retain explicit level/path/layer/solidity ownership rather than global
  player-query fallback.
- Engine/Loop ports preserve contextual provider/proxy authentication and exact
  operands, four native modes, live capture and completion/fade/PLC ordering.
  Results forwarding uses the existing special-stage lifecycle owner; it does not
  call the native provider's deliberately throwing context-free default or create
  a shared-game-to-S3K cycle. Bonus fixtures supply their actual owned context.
  Audio playlist admission leases coexist with native transport and exact backend
  identity. The separate recording detach and its regression remain unchanged.
  Strict zero concrete Sonic references, the incoming 3305 Loop budget, 3073 OM
  budget and the actual candidate API additions/union remain enforced; no cap,
  pin, version or guard baseline was relaxed.

constructor before reaching their directed-body oracles. The final failure was
an unchanged monolithic release expectation; its original G1 kind/remove operands
were UNKNOWN. G1 nevertheless executed real EGL ownership/shadow/replay pixels
(23), API/signature/pin/release/hook cases (77), actual native FIFO/repeat/ID-zero
controls and other selected cases. Those positives remain on `44ce2b13`.

### Reproducible command and selector shapes

The following records completed commands, not new execution authority. Work from
`<PR_WORKTREE>`; set `OPENGGF_QUEUE_WRAPPER` to
`<MAIN_WORKSPACE>/tools/testing/maven_queue.py` and `OPENGGF_ROM_DIR` to the existing
stock-ROM directory. Use JDK 25 and Lua 5.4 via `JAVA_HOME=<JDK25_HOME>` and
`LUA_BIN=<LUA54_BIN>`, `ALSOFT_DRIVERS=null`, `EGL_PLATFORM=surfaceless`, and
`PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`. Unset `JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS`, `JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS`,
`_JAVA_OPTIONS`, `MAVEN_OPTS`, `JAVA_OPTS`, `MAVEN_ARGS`, `DISPLAY`,
`WAYLAND_DISPLAY`, `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`, `LIBGL_DRIVERS_PATH`,
`LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE` and `MESA_LOADER_DRIVER_OVERRIDE`. No heap/fork/resource
or timeout override was added. The recorded Main wrapper SHA was
`77d3a3e53dfc213729a57b41d5d11dc1b2145a158a01fa0ca6313a61c3639720`, with its ten
local import-module pins captured for attribution; no duplicate version/preflight
probe was run. Later suites report Java 25.0.5.0.2 and class version 69.

```bash
# Closure builds, no ROM properties, -Dtest or test goal:
python3 "$OPENGGF_QUEUE_WRAPPER" --normal -Dmse=off \
  -Dopenggf.headless.gl=surfaceless-egl test-compile -B

# Actual Python safety request, from <PR_WORKTREE>/tools/testing:
PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python3 -m unittest \
  test_run_category_small test_run_category_small_profile test_run_category_dashboard

# JUnit shape; SELECTOR is the comma-joined block below, with no extra members.
# G3 and POLICY additionally include -Pguards in a separate fresh JVM.
python3 "$OPENGGF_QUEUE_WRAPPER" --normal -Dmse=off \
  -Dopenggf.headless.gl=surfaceless-egl -DmodApi.destinationBranch=develop \
  "-Dtest=$SELECTOR" \
  "-Dsonic1.rom.path=$OPENGGF_ROM_DIR/Sonic The Hedgehog (W) (REV01) [!].gen" \
  "-Dsonic2.rom.path=$OPENGGF_ROM_DIR/Sonic The Hedgehog 2 (W) (REV01) [!].gen" \
  "-Ds3k.rom.path=$OPENGGF_ROM_DIR/Sonic and Knuckles & Sonic 3 (W) [!].gen" test -B
```

## 2026-10-24 — native ownership on renderbuffer-depth scene targets

This bounded leaf starts from sole parent
`3da9b979538ece909ad5d39a82f58d2c9b417534` in isolated worktree
`<task-worktree>`, branch
`bugfix/ai-pr223-native-depth-stencil-20261024`. The parent supplied two original
3da errors in
`TestStageplayLiveWorldRendering#nativeBodiesAppearAtTheResolvedDirectorCoordinatesWithoutTicking(String)`:
`[4]` (`air`, `live-air-traffic`) failed on the frame-115 draw at original test
line 49; `[5]` (`boundary`, `one-more-take`) failed on its initial frame-35 draw
at original line 41, before its frame-1375 and frame-2255 draws. Both reported
`NativeSpriteOwnership.requireComplete` status `0x8CDD` (`UNSUPPORTED`). This is
supplied reproduction evidence, not a new baseline run or proof of newness or a
particular driver's cause. The parent's broad source, targets and jobs were not
read, changed or observed by this leaf.

Source inspection identified the concrete portability gap: Stageplay's private
tile target uses `FboHelper.createWithDepth`, an RGBA8 colour texture plus a D16
depth renderbuffer without stencil or multisampling. Native ownership previously
left that depth image attached while appending a separate S8 image. Simultaneous
depth and stencil support is guaranteed for the same packed image, not that
separate D16/S8 combination. Retaining separate S8 on this caller was therefore
rejected; the observed unsupported status is consistent with that gap without
establishing its exact driver cause.

For the no-stencil, depth-renderbuffer case only, ownership now captures the
foreign renderbuffer identity, width, height and sample count, allocates one
owned D24S8 image, and attaches it to both depth and stencil. Depth testing and
writes remain disabled during native drawing. The foreign image is detached,
never cleared, copied, reallocated or drawn into; normal close, queued draw
failure and begin failure restore its exact attachment and remove scratch
stencil. The changed-attachment flag is set before either installation call.
Colour-only S8 and existing-stencil backup/format/copy paths, native FIFO,
first-opaque ownership, ghost order, palette masks, SHADOW_15 and SAT budgets
retain their previous behavior. No shared FBO helper or public API changed.

The two added cases in `TestNativeSpriteOwnershipPixels` were authored before
the production edit:

- `depthRenderbufferTargetsPreservePixelsDepthAndStateAfterDrawAndQueuedFailure`
  uses real `createWithDepth(352,256)` with a 320×224 viewport, ordinary native
  first-opaque drawing and both live/published ghost replay. It checks original
  depth/colour attachment identity, full-image depth bytes with distinct
  off-viewport sentinels, packed scratch dimensions and single-sample metadata,
  bindings, read/draw buffers, viewport and graphics state after success and
  queued draw failure, plus deletion of the captured scratch object names.
- `depthRenderbufferAndStateSurviveBeginFailureAfterScratchInstallation`
  deliberately injects `GL_INVALID_ENUM`; it requires the exact
  `clearing native SAT coverage: GL error 0x500` failure after scratch installation
  and verifies original attachment, depth bytes and state restoration. This is
  an asserted negative control, not an unexpected validation error. Existing
  injected draw failures and shadow validation/producer failures likewise remain
  source-asserted negative controls.

The Stageplay fixture now calls `GraphicsManager.cleanup()` in a local finally
inside its boot try-with-resources, after private world/readback resources close
and while its context is current. Removing only this wrapper reconstructs the
entire old test file exactly: all five `first`, `hud`, `platform`, `air`,
`boundary` parameters, frame bounds (0; 35; 35; 115; 35→1375→2255), coordinates
and assertions are unchanged. Removing the added pixel cases/helpers and expanded
state snapshot likewise reconstructs the old pixel test file exactly.
